> *   **Compatibility:** This endpoint allows you to use the standard OpenAI SDK (Python, TypeScript/JavaScript) to interact with Gemini models[1][3].

> *   **Models:** Make sure the `model` parameter is set to a valid, supported Gemini model (e.g., `gemini-2.0-flash`, `gemini-1.5-pro`)[1][5].

> The error message **"is not found for API version v1main"** typically indicates that the application or integration you are using is attempting to access a Gemini model via a deprecated or incorrect API endpoint (`v1main`)[1][2].
> 
> This error often arises in third-party tools (like Cursor, various CLI agents, or custom integrations) that have not been updated to use the current Google Generative Language API endpoints (such as `v1beta` or `v1`)[1].
> 
> ### Common Causes & Solutions
> 
> *   **Incorrect or Deprecated Model Name:** You may be trying to use a model name that does not exist, has been deprecated, or is misspelled (e.g., trying to use `gemini-2.5-pro-max` when the API only recognizes `gemini-2.5-pro-exp` or a specific stable version)[2][3][4].
>     *   **Fix:** Check the official list of available models. You can often see which models are available for your API key by calling the `ListModels` method via the Google AI Studio API or checking the [Google AI documentation](https://aistudio.google.com/)[3][5][4].
> *   **Outdated Software/Integration:** If you are using a tool like Cursor or a GitHub project, the application itself may be hardcoded to use the `v1main` endpoint, which Google has phased out[1][3][4].
>     *   **Fix:** Update the application to the latest version. If the developer has not updated the tool, you may need to look for an alternative configuration or file a bug report on their repository[1][3].
> *   **Modality/Endpoint Mismatch:** Some errors occur when forcing a "text-only" request through an endpoint or model designed for "Live" (audio/video/bidi) interactions[2][5].
>     *   **Fix:** Ensure your code is using the correct endpoint for your use case (e.g., `streamGenerateContent` for text instead of `bidiGenerateContent` for live sessions)[2].
> *   **Custom API Configurations:** If you are using a tool that allows you to set a `base_ur
> <truncated 576 bytes>
>  code, ensure your request base URL does not include `/v1main/` and is instead using the standard `/v1/` or `/v1beta/` paths.

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Observing Streaming Implementation**

I've been looking closely at the `generate-content` function. I see it smartly handles API key selection, defaulting to Groq or OpenRouter. The key thing I've spotted is how it uses streaming, returning a Response object. Crucially, the content type is explicitly set to `text/event-stream`.


**Refining Fallback Implementation**

I'm now zeroing in on the fallback strategy for streaming scenarios. Deno's `fetch` seems ideal, and I've confirmed it supports streaming responses. The elegance lies in the early check: a non-2xx status on the initial response allows us to seamlessly intercept and switch to a secondary provider (like Gemini) before any chunks are streamed. I'm currently designing the parameters for the shared `fetchWithFallback` wrapper function, thinking about a more general approach.


**Implementing Fallback Logic**

I'm now building out the logic for payment or rate limit errors from OpenRouter/Groq. I'm developing a fallback strategy, creating `supabase/functions/_shared/llm-fallback.ts` to manage potential issues with OpenAI/OpenRouter chat completions in `orchestrate-step`. This helper will export some core functionalities.


**Implementing Fallback Logic**

I'm now integrating a `fetchWithFallback` function within `llm-fallback.ts`. This involves checking the response status codes (402, 429) from OpenRouter/Groq to detect credit exhaustion or rate limiting. I'm also addressing key handling, ensuring both `GEMINI_API_KEY` and `VITE_GEMINI_API_KEY` are read from the environment safely, using a conditional fallback.


**Refining Fallback Mechanics**

I'm now refining the fallback mechanism within `llm-fallback.ts`. Specifically, I'm enhancing credit error detection to include checks within the response body, even if the status code isn't a direct 402 or 429. I'm also ensuring a graceful fallback to Gemini, including robust key handling, parsing the request body safely, and mapping the original model to a suitable Gemini equivalent. Moreover, the gemini models will attempt a priority cascade.


**Prioritizing Model Cascade**

I'm now implementing a priority cascade within the Gemini fallback. I've defined ordered model arrays based on the original model requested, such as prioritizing `gemini-2.5-pro` or `gemini-2.5-flash`. The code iterates through these models, constructing and sending requests with the proper body. Errors, including 404, will be handled to allow the process to move to the next preferred model, or return the original error.

> 1: # SOP — SQL Bridge Admin Bootstrap
> 2: 
> 3: **Versão:** v1 · **Selada:** 2026-05-16 · **Lei 2 (Processo Antecipado)**
> 4: 
> 5: ## ORO triplet
> 6: 
> 7: - **Operator:** Sovereign (Gabriel) executando direto via Supabase Studio SQL Editor
> 8: - **Reviewer:** Sovereign (self-review — sensível, sem delegação)
> 9: - **Owner:** Sovereign (security boundary change)
> 10: 
> 11: ## Contexto
> 12: 
> 13: `aios-sql-bridge` v1 requer admin role (`has_role(auth.uid(),'admin')`). Audit v1 mostrou que `user_roles` só contém roles `viewer` — nenhum admin existe. Sem admin, end-to-end do bridge é inacessível. Esta SOP é o **bootstrap manual seguro** para promover o Sovereign user.
> 14: 
> 15: ## Pre-conditions
> 16: 
> 17: - Sovereign user_id: `ada39fae-67e1-4e53-af1c-5a18e1c108e8` (confirmar em `profiles`)
> 18: - Acesso ao Supabase Studio (https://supabase.com/dashboard/project/bcyvddsykvehvpwstlfa)
> 19: - `user_roles` tem RLS — service-role bypass apenas via Studio SQL Editor ou via psql com service-role key
> 20: 
> 21: ## Sequence — execução manual humana
> 22: 
> 23: | # | Action | Output esperado | Verification gate |
> 24: |---|--------|-----------------|-------------------|
> 25: | 1 | Confirmar Sovereign user_id via `SELECT id, mco_balance FROM profiles ORDER BY mco_balance DESC LIMIT 5` | UUID `ada39fae...` no topo (highest balance) | Match string-by-string |
> 26: | 2 | Verificar app_role enum: `SELECT unnest(enum_range(NULL::app_role))` | Lista contém `'admin'` | `'admin'` presente |
> 27: | 3 | Confirmar que `ada39fae...` ainda não é admin: `SELECT * FROM user_rol
> <truncated 890 bytes>
> E retornar o UUID. Se erro de duplicata, rollback (step 3 falhou) — investigar.
> 36: - Step 5: `has_role` retorna `true`. Se `false`, RLS pode estar bloqueando OR role não foi inserido. Verificar com `SELECT * FROM user_roles WHERE user_id=...`.
> 37: - Step 7: HTTP 200 com row real. Se 403 "Admin role required", role não foi propagado (cache JWT?). Sovereign refresh do JWT (logout/login).
> 38: 
> 39: ## Recovery path
> 40: 
> 41: - **Step 4 falhou (RLS bloqueia INSERT)**: rodar via service-role no SQL Editor (Studio bypasses RLS). Se ainda falhar, ALTER TABLE user_roles DISABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY temporário (super-risky, reverter imediatamente após insert).
> 42: - **Step 7 retorna 401 "Invalid JWT"**: JWT expirou. Re-logar e tentar com token novo. JWT do Supabase tem ~1h de validade.
> 43: - **Step 7 retorna 429 "Daily quota exceeded"**: já consumiu 100 queries hoje. Esperar 24h OU `DELETE FROM usage_tracking WHERE user_id=... AND resource_type='sql_bridge_query' AND created_at > NOW() - INTERVAL '24h'` para reset.
> 44: 
> 45: ## Success signal
> 46: 
> 47: - `SELECT * FROM user_roles WHERE user_id='ada39fae...' AND role='admin'` retorna 1 row
> 48: - Curl POST com JWT retorna `{"rows":[{...}], "rowCount":1, "queryHash":"...", "executedAt":"..."}`
> 49: - `infra_health_logs` mostra entry `service='aios-sql-bridge'`, status='healthy', timestamp ≤ 5s atrás
> 50: 
> 51: ## Anti-patterns
> 52: 
> 53: - ❌ Promover múltiplos users a admin "preventivamente" — apenas Sovereign até Phase Commercial
> 54: - ❌ Deixar SQL Editor aberto com admin role após o smoke — fechar imediatamente
> 55: - ❌ Compartilhar JWT em chat — JWT é credencial; pode ser revogado mas custa fricção

> 1: # SOP — Canvas Video Async Execution
> 2: 
> 3: **Versão:** v1 · **Selada:** 2026-05-17 · **Lei 2 (Processo Antecipado)** · **Phase 4.2c**
> 4: 
> 5: ## ORO triplet
> 6: 
> 7: - **Operator:** end user (gera vídeo no Canvas Studio); admin (override manual em job stuck)
> 8: - **Reviewer:** Sovereign (aprova consumo de hf credits + valida saldo Higgsfield no dashboard)
> 9: - **Owner:** Sovereign (Pillar 3 cost discipline + dono dos ~30 hf credits pagos)
> 10: 
> 11: ## Contexto
> 12: 
> 13: A geração de vídeo via Higgsfield (DoP / Kling / Seedance) leva 20–120s — excede o teto de 90s do polling síncrono dentro de Edge Function. Para Phase 4.2c, o `canvas-execute` dispatcha jobs de `image_to_video` num caminho **async fire-and-forget**: insere row em `vm_canvas_executions` com `status='queued'`, submete à Higgsfield com `?hf_webhook=<callback>` query param, retorna imediatamente; a Higgsfield POSTa de volta no `higgsfield-webhook` quando o vídeo termina; o webhook baixa o vídeo (≥100 KB + video/* content-type), upload pro `canvas-assets` bucket, debita mcoCoins **só após upload OK**, INSERT em `vm_canvas_assets`. `useCanvasJobsRealtime` faz a UI re-renderizar via Supabase Realtime sem refresh.
> 14: 
> 15: **Por que existe:** Sem este pipeline, o Canvas não consegue produzir vídeo end-to-end → bloqueia validação do flywheel Higgsfield → bloqueia revenue via afiliados ML. Phase 4.2c foi desbloqueada por OE04 (v6.6.0) que subiu Cost Discipline para 4/5 + SOP para 5/5.
> 16: 
> 17: **Catálogo conservador (Sovereign decidiu 2026-05-17):** apenas `d
> <truncated 6391 bytes>
> vas-video-watchdog')` com `status='healthy'` no happy path.
> 107: 
> 108: ## Anti-patterns
> 109: 
> 110: - ❌ **Polling síncrono dentro de canvas-execute para video** — excede 90s edge function timeout, queima crédito sem entregar URL.
> 111: - ❌ **Deduzir mcoCoins antes do upload OK** — quebra invariante "atomic só-após-upload-OK"; se download falha, user paga por nada.
> 112: - ❌ **Webhook token reutilizado entre executions** — quebra defesa contra replay; migration garante `UNIQUE INDEX` em `webhook_token`.
> 113: - ❌ **Skip do health pulse em failure paths** — observabilidade quebrada; SSP-01 OE03 Pillar 4 requer best-effort pulse mesmo em erro.
> 114: - ❌ **Run-All bloqueante esperando vídeo terminar** — UX morre. Fire-and-forget: submit retorna, Realtime atualiza depois.
> 115: - ❌ **Habilitar Lite/Turbo/Kling/Seedance sem confirmar endpoint** — gasta hf credits em 404. Manter `dop-standard-5s` único até Phase 4.3.
> 116: 
> 117: ## Referências

> The following code has been modified to include a line number before every line, in the format: <line_number>: <original_line>. Please note that any changes targeting the original code should remove the line number, colon, and leading space.
> 1: import { useEffect } from 'react';
> 2: import { useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
> 3: import { supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client';
> 4: import { useAuth } from '@/hooks/useAuth';
> 5: 
> 6: /**
> 7:  * vm_canvas_executions row shape relayed by the Realtime callback. Includes
> 8:  * the columns Phase 4.2c needs to reconcile the Zustand store on the client.
> 9:  */
> 10: export interface CanvasExecutionRow {
> 11:   id: string;
> 12:   project_id: string;
> 13:   node_id: string | null;
> 14:   status: 'pending' | 'queued' | 'running' | 'success' | 'failed' | 'cancelled' | 'timeout';
> 15:   output_url: string | null;
> 16:   error_message: string | null;
> 17:   completed_at: string | null;
> 18: }
> 19: 
> 20: /**
> 21:  * Subscribes to vm_canvas_executions UPDATEs for the current user via Supabase Realtime.
> 22:  * Invalidates TanStack Query keys on every status transition AND invokes the optional
> 23:  * `onUpdate` callback so callers can reconcile the Zustand canvas store with the row.
> 24:  *
> 25:  * Used by Phase 4.2c async video flow: higgsfield-webhook flips status
> 26:  * `queued → success/failed/timeout`, this hook (a) refetches query caches and
> 27:  * (b) delegates the node-level status sync to the caller via `onUpdate`.
> 28:  *
> 29:  * Pattern mirrors `useMcorchMemory.ts:91-112` (canonical Realtime subscription).
> 30:  * RLS-aligned: `user_id=eq.<auth.uid>` filter ensures cross-tenant isolation at
> 31:  * the channel level. The callback is the single bridge between Realtime → Zustand,
> 32:  * keeping the store as the source of truth for node visual state.
> 33:  */
> 34: export function useCanvasJobsRealtime(
> 35:   projectId?: string,
> 36:   onUpdate?: (row: CanvasExecutionRow) => void,
> 37: ): void {
> 38:   const qc = useQueryClient();
> 39:   const { user } = useAuth();
> 40: 
> 41:   useEffect(() => {
> 42:     if (!user) return;
> 43: 
> 44:     const channel = supabase
> 45:       .channel(`canvas_jobs_${user.id}`)
> 46:       .on(
> 47:         'postgres_changes',
> 48:         {
> 49:           event: 'UPDATE',
> 50:           schema: 'public',
> 51:           table: 'vm_canvas_executions',
> 52:           filter: `user_id=eq.${user.id}`,
> 53:         },
> 54:         (payload) => {
> 55:           const row = payload.new as CanvasExecutionRow;
> 56:           qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['canvas-execution', row.id] });
> 57:           if (projectId && row.project_id === projectId) {
> 58:             qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['vm-canvas-assets', projectId] });
> 59:             qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['vm-canvas-executions', projectId] });
> 60:           }
> 61:           onUpdate?.(row);
> 62:         },
> 63:       )
> 64:       .subscribe();
> 65: 
> 66:     return () => {
> 67:       supabase.removeChannel(channel);
> 68:     };
> 69:   }, [user?.id, projectId, qc, onUpdate]);
> 70: }
> 71: 
> The above content shows the entire, complete file contents of the requested file.
> 
> ```

<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Evaluating Script Completeness**

I've reviewed the `scripts/canvas-video-watchdog.sh` script; it's robust and aligns well with our SOP, incorporating a circuit breaker and comprehensive health status logging. It seems solid. For Phase 4.2c, my focus is now on updating `canvas-execute/index.ts` to accommodate the new permitted models, and then performing rigorous watchdog testing to ensure it's functioning as expected.


**Designing Fallback Mechanism**

I'm now fully immersed in designing a robust fallback mechanism. I'm building a Deno shared helper, `supabase/functions/_shared/llm-fallback.ts`, to handle 402/429 errors and timeouts from OpenRouter, falling back to Gemini Free or OpenRouter Free. I'm defining the LLM cascade: heavy models cascade to Gemini Pro/1.5 Pro, and medium/light models cascade to Gemini Flash/1.5 Flash. I'll include OpenRouter Free in the cascade using its `:free` models. The helper will expose a function for easy integration.


**Defining Helper Architecture**

I'm now detailing the structure of the fallback helper. I plan to define the `LLMPayload` and `LLMOptions` structures. The helper will handle `fetch` calls to the `aiEndpoint` with the relevant headers (Authorization, Content-Type, etc.). It will receive the original endpoint, the OpenRouter and Gemini API keys, and the chat completions payload. I've examined `supabase/functions/generate-content/index.ts` to grasp the call format. I'm exploring Deno's handling of local imports in Edge Functions, specifically, how Supabase handles them during deploy.


**Refining API Endpoint Strategy**

I've clarified the Gemini OpenAI-compatible endpoint usage: it simplifies things since the payload mirrors the standard OpenAI format, only needing a changed `model` field and the `Authorization` header. I'm focusing on defining a test plan to confirm this URL works with streams and regular responses. I'm preparing to draft an SOP for LLM fallback at `docs/processes/llm-cascading-fallback.md` too.


**Drafting Implementation Plan**

I'm now prioritizing the creation of `implementation_plan.md` in the artifacts directory. I'll need to generate this file and ensure it undergoes user approval. I'm also preparing to create the necessary directories using `write_to_file`. To align with "Law 2", the SOP for LLM fallback in `docs/processes/llm-cascading-fallback.md` will be documented, and then I can refine the design of `llm-fallback.ts`.

</details>

  "CodeContent": "\"# SOP \u2014 LLM Cascading Fallback\\n\\n**Vers\u00e3o:** v1 \u00b7 **Selada:** 2026-05-31 \u00b7 **Lei 2 (Processo Antecipado)** \u00b7 **OTD-LLM-FALLBACK**\\n\\n## ORO triplet\\n\\n- **Operator:** MCORCH Master Execution Agent (executa nas Edge Functions de completions)\\n- **Reviewer:** Sovereign (Gabriel)\\n- **Owner:** Sovereign (Gabriel) - Blast radius de custos e confiabilidade da gera\u00e7\u00e3o de conte\u00fado.\\n\\n## Contexto\\n\\nA orquestra\u00e7\u00e3o do Constellation Orchestra depende de completions confi\u00e1veis de LLM para gerar artigos, scripts, posts e planos. Falling back to Gemini Free.\\\" |\\n| 3 | Helper mapeia o modelo para Gemini correspondente (Heavy vs Light/Medium) | Modelo definido: `gemini-2.5-pro` / `gemini-1.5-pro` ou `gemini-2.5-flash` / `gemini-1.5-flash` | Mapeamento no log de depura\u00e7\u00e3o do Deno |\\n| 4 | Helper faz chamada secund\u00e1ria ao Gemini OpenAI-Compatible Endpoint com a chave de API resolvida | Retorna HTTP 200 com a resposta do Gemini em formato compat\u00edvel com OpenAI (incluindo streams) | `Authorization` header cont\u00e9m `Bearer <GEMINI_API_KEY>`; corpo da resposta tem formato OpenAI |\\n| 5 | Se chamada ao Gemini falhar (HTTP diferente de 2xx ou rede offline) | Helper intercepta e\n<truncated 2039 bytes>",

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Designing the Fallback Helper**

I'm now focused on designing the `supabase/functions/_shared/llm-fallback.ts` helper. I'll need to handle that the `_shared` directory doesn't exist yet, so it should be created via `write_to_file`. The fallback implementation itself is the current focus, which will receive the original endpoint URL.


**Defining Error Handling Logic**

Now I'm focusing on error handling in the initial call and the fallback cascade. Specifically, how to detect 402/429 errors from the initial request (using `isFallbackTriggerStatus` function), and how to handle `fetch` failures via `try/catch`. The fallback cascade will be triggered if the request fails, beginning with Gemini Free if configured, extracting the original model.


**Classifying Model Weights**

I've been working on a system to classify model weights, using regex to distinguish between "heavy" and "medium/light" models based on their names. The initial classification considers models such as Claude 3.5, GPT-4 and those with a high parameter count to be "heavy". I'm still figuring out specific edge cases, like how `meta-llama/llama-3.3-70b-instruct` should be classified. The plan is to map "heavy" to `gemini-2.5-pro` (then `gemini-1.5-pro` if that fails), and others to `gemini-2.5-flash` (then `gemini-1.5-flash`).

</details>

  "CodeContent": "\"// supabase/functions/_shared/llm-fallback.ts\\n// Shared helper for OTD-LLM-FALLBACK. Falls back gracefully when OpenRouter credits are exhausted.\\n\\nexport interface FallbackOptions {\\n  openRouterKey?: string;\\n  geminiKey?: string;\\n  groqKey?: string;\\n}\\n\\nconst HEAVY_MODEL_REGEX = /claude-3\\\\.5|claude-4|gpt-4|llama-3\\\\.1-405b/i;\\n\\n/**\\n * Executes a chat completion fetch request, falling back gracefully to free alternatives\\n * if the primary provider fails due to credit exhaustion (402), rate-limits (429), or network errors.\\n * \\n * Cascade order:\\n * 1. Primary provider (OpenRouter / Groq)\\n * 2. Gemini Free OpenAI-Compatible Endpoint:\\n *    - Heavy models -> gemini-2.5-pro -> gemini-1.5-pro\\n *    - Medium/Light models -> gemini-2.5-flash -> gemini-1.5-flash\\n * 3. OpenRouter Free (gemma-2-9b-it:free / llama-3-8b-instruct:free)\\n */\\nexport async function fetchLLMWithFallback(\\n  originalEndpoint: string,\\n  requestInit: RequestInit,\\n  options: FallbackOptions\\n): Promise<Response> {\\n  const openRouterKey = options.openRouterKey;\\n  const geminiKey = options.geminiKey;\\n  const groqKey = options.groqKey;\\n\\n  let requestBodyObj: any = {};\\n  if (requestInit.body && typeof requestInit.body === \\\"string\\\") {\\n    try {\\n      requestBodyObj = JSON.parse(requestInit.body);\\n    } catch (_) {\\n      console.warn(\\\"[llm-fallback] Failed to parse request body as JSON\\\");\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  const originalModel = requestBodyObj.model || \\\"\\\";\\n\\n  // Helper to execute fetch and handle potential network errors\\n  const safeFetch = async (url: string, init: RequestInit): Promise<Response> => {\\n    try {\\n      return await fetch(url, init);\\n    } catch (err) {\\n      console.warn(`[llm-fallback] Fetch error to ${url}:`, err);\\n      // Return a synthetic 500 response to trigger the next level of cascade\\n      return new Response(\\n        JSON.stringify({ error: \\\"Network fetch failed\\\", details: String(err) }),\\n        { status: 500, headers: { \\\"Content-Type\\\": \\\"application/json\\\" } }\\n      );\\n    }\\n\n<truncated 4728 bytes>",

> 1133: Regras cruciais:
> 1134: 1. Retorne APENAS o prompt final expandido em inglês (ideal para os geradores de imagem/vídeo).
> 1135: 2. Não adicione observações, explicações, aspas extras ou introduções como "Here is your prompt:". Retorne apenas o texto do prompt.
> 1136: 3. Se o modelo de destino for de vídeo (Higgsfield, Seedance, etc.), descreva o movimento da câmera, ações e efeitos temporais.
> 1137: 4. Utilize o contexto da campanha (se fornecido) para alinhar o estilo do prompt com a marca/audiência.
> 1138: 5. NUNCA inclua instruções de comportamento, definições de papel, ou estruturas de saída no output expandido. Proibido: "You are X", "Your function is Y", "Output ONLY", "IDENTITY & PROTOCOL CONTEXT", "Return only JSON", listas numeradas de regras, blocos de código. O output deve ser PURA DESCRIÇÃO VISUAL (substantivos + adjetivos + cenário + iluminação + estilo) — modelos de imagem leem qualquer estrutura instrucional como "gere texto" e devolvem texto em vez de imagem.`;

> 1: # Plano de Implementação — OTD-LLM-FALLBACK, aios-sql-bridge & Canvas Studio Phase 4.2c
> 2: 
> 3: Este plano detalha os passos para implementar o fallback em cascata de completions das Edge Functions, validar a segurança e funcionamento da SQL Bridge e finalizar a fase 4.2c de vídeo assíncrono no Canvas Studio.
> 4: 
> 5: ## User Review Required
> 6: 
> 7: > [!IMPORTANT]
> 8: > - O fallback do OpenRouter para o Gemini Free utilizará a URL oficial compatível com a API da OpenAI do Gemini (`/v1beta/openai/chat/completions`). Isso nos permite usar a mesma payload (incluindo JSON Schema e structured outputs) sem precisar convertê-la para o formato do Google AI SDK.
> 9: > - O fallback detectará falhas baseadas em HTTP status `402` (créditos insuficientes no OpenRouter), `429` (rate-limit) ou erros gerais de rede.
> 10: > - A ativação dos modelos de vídeo no `canvas-execute` permitirá executar qualquer modelo suportado pelo catálogo Higgsfield (DoP Lite, DoP Turbo, Kling, Seedance) em ambiente de execução assíncrono.

> 136:         const { error: updErr } = await admin
> 137:           .from("profiles").update({ status }).eq("id", targetUserId);
> 138:         if (updErr) throw updErr;
> 139:         result = { status };
> 140:         break;
> 141:       }
> 142: 
> 143:       case "hard_delete": {
> 144:         // Cascade every record the target owns, then remove the auth user.
> 145:         const { error: rpcErr } = await admin.rpc("delete_account", { p_user_id: targetUserId });
> 146:         if (rpcErr) throw rpcErr;
> 147:         const { error: authDelErr } = await admin.auth.admin.deleteUser(targetUserId);
> 148:         if (authDelErr) console.error("[admin-manage-user] auth delete:", authDelErr.message);
> 149:         result = { hard_deleted: true, auth_deleted: !authDelErr };
> 150:         break;
> 151:       }
> 152: 
> 153:       default:
> 154:         return json({ error: "Ação desconhecida" }, 422);
> 155:     }
> 156: 
> 157:     // 4. Audit trail — best-effort; never fails the action it records.
> 158:     const { error: auditErr } = await admin.from("audit_logs").insert({
> 159:       user_id: user.id,
> 160:       action: `admin_${action}`,
> 161:       resource_type: "user",
> 162:       resource_id: targetUserId,
> 163:       details: { ...result, by_admin: user.email },
> 164:     });
> 165:     if (auditErr) console.error("[admin-manage-user] audit insert failed:", auditErr.message);
> 166: 
> 167:     return json({ status: "ok", action, target_user_id: targetUserId, ...result });
> 168:   } catch (e) {
> 169:     const message = e instanceof Error ? e.message : "Erro interno";
> 170:     console.error("[admin-manage-user] error:", message);
> 171:     await admin.from("infra_health_logs").insert({
> 172:       service: "admin-manage-user",
> 173:       status: "error",
> 174:       last_seen_at: new Date().toISOString(),
> 175:     }).then(() => {}, () => {});
> 176:     return json({ error: message }, 500);
> 177:   }
> 178: });

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Analyzing Authentication Methods**

I'm currently focused on how `admin-manage-user` handles user authentication. I've noted the use of JWT for caller identification and the subsequent role check to verify admin privileges. It seems the system relies on the caller's JWT and a role-based access control approach for authorization.


**Refining Access Control Logic**

I've just confirmed the expected behavior: `has_role` checks are tied to the caller's JWT, not the admin client used by `aios-sql-bridge`. This means the existing call is failing because the function expects a single argument (`_role`), but it's receiving the wrong arguments (`_user_id` and `_role`). My next step is to modify the Edge Function `aios-sql-bridge` to properly verify admin roles. I'm considering two solutions: using the user's JWT or a direct database query.


**Evaluating Direct Database Query**

I'm now leaning towards a direct database query on the `user_roles` table using the `admin` client, finding it more efficient and reliable. I've confirmed `public.user_roles` exists and is accessible. This approach offers a deterministic and secure admin role check. However, I'm considering the implications of the "Law 2" SOP rule before implementing the change, even though it appears to be a bug fix related to a pre-approved implementation plan.

</details>

## 🛠️ Resumo de Ações Executadas

1.  **Correção do CheckAdmin (`aios-sql-bridge`)**: Alteramos a lógica de validação do administrador para ler diretamente a tabela `user_roles` com o cliente `admin` (service role). A chamada anterior tentava invocar a RPC `has_role` com dois parâmetros (`_user_id`, `_role`) que não existe mais após as migrações que unificaram a assinatura do `has_role(_role)` baseado em `auth.uid()`.
2.  **Deploy Geral**: Realizamos o deploy com sucesso de todas as 12 Edge Functions afetadas no projeto Supabase `bcyvddsykvehvpwstlfa`.
3.  **Smoke Test com Vitest**: Rodamos o teste de fumaça (`bun run test src/test/aios-sql-bridge-smoke.test.ts`) em produção e ele validou:
    *   **CASE 1 (Happy Path)**: Retornou HTTP `200` e o saldo mcoCoins correto.
    *   **CASE 2 (Forbidden Column)**: Bloqueou leitura do campo `email` com HTTP `400`.
    *   **CASE 3 (Non-whitelisted Table)**: Barrou acesso à tabela `decrypted_user_api_keys` com HTTP `400`.
4.  **Watchdog Script**: Executamos localmente `bash scripts/canvas-video-watchdog.sh` validando a ausência de jobs travados e a gravação de logs de telemetria saudável (`infra_health_logs`).
5.  **Granular Commits**: Sincronizamos o histórico com dois commits específicos:
    *   `57d34e130b0cebda9d67fd2e1022ff80a295f9fb` — Implementação e integração do fallback de LLM.
    *   `af3abdb88b0aaa20b7b577801ac8f1661823efd1` — Correção do gate do admin e testes de fumaça da SQL Bridge.
